Huleihel 2018
, to simultaneously map
{\rm{Bern}}(p)
distribution to
N(\xi,1)
and
{\rm{Bern}}(q)
distribution to
N(0,1)
approximately.

•

Step 2
: Simultaneously change the magnitude and sparsity of the planted signal guided by the target problem. In this step, we develop several new techniques and apply several ones in the literature. In
{\rm{CHC}}_{D}
(Algorithm
10
), we use the average-trick idea in
Ma and Wu 2015
; in
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
(Algorithm
11
), we use the invariant property of Gaussian to handle the multiway-symmetricity of hypergraph; to achieve a sharper scaling of signal strength and sparsity in
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D},{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
(Algorithm
8
), the
tensor reflection cloning
, a generalization of reflection cloning
(
Brennan, Bresler and
Huleihel 2018
)
, is introduced that spreads the signal in the planted high-order cluster along each mode evenly, maintains the independence of entries in the tensor, and only mildly reduces the signal magnitude.

•

Step 3
: Randomly permute indices of different modes to transform the symmetric planted signal tensor to an asymmetric one (Lemmas 14 and 16 in
Luo and Zhang 2020b
) that maps to the high-order clustering problem.

Then, we give a detailed proof of Theorem
17
, i.e., computational lower bounds for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
. The proofs for the computational limits of
{\rm{CHC}}_{D}
and
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
are similar and postponed to the supplementary materials
(
Luo and Zhang 2020b
)
.

We first introduce the rejection kernel scheme given in Algorithm 9 in
Luo and Zhang 2020b
Section C, which simultaneously maps
{\rm{Bern}}(p)
to distribution
f_{X}
and
{\rm{Bern}}(q)
to distribution
g_{X}
approximately. In our high-order clustering problem,
f_{X}
and
g_{X}
are
N(\xi,1)
and
N(0,1)
, i.e., the distribution of the entries inside and outside the planted cluster, respectively. Here,
\xi
is to be specified later. Denote
{\rm{RK}}(p\to f_{X},q\to g_{X},T)
as the rejection kernel map, where
T
is the number of iterations in the rejection kernel algorithm.

We then propose a new tensor reflection cloning technique in Algorithm
7
. Note that the input tensor
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{0}
to Algorithm
7
often has independent entries and a sparse planted cluster, we multiply
{\mathbfcal{W}}^{\sigma^{\otimes d}}
, a random permutation of
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{0}
, by
\frac{{\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{B}}}{\sqrt{2}}
in each mode to “spread" the signal of the planted cluster along all modes while keep the entries independent. We prove some properties related to tensor reflection cloning in Lemma 16 of
Luo and Zhang 2020b
Section C.

Algorithm 7

Tensor Reflecting Cloning

1:

Input:
Tensor
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes d}}
(
n
is an even number), number of iterations
\ell
.

2:

Initialize
{\mathbfcal{W}}={\mathbfcal{W}}_{0}
.

3:

For
i=1,\ldots,\ell
, do:

(a)

Generate a permutation
\sigma
of
[n]
uniformly at random.

(b)

Calculate

{\mathbfcal{W}}^{\prime}={\mathbfcal{W}}^{\sigma^{\otimes d}}\times_{1}\frac{{\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{B}}}{\sqrt{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}\frac{{\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{B}}}{\sqrt{2}},

where
{\mathbfcal{W}}^{\sigma^{\otimes d}}
means permuting each mode indices of
{\mathbfcal{W}}
by
\sigma
and
{\mathbf{B}}
is a
n\times n
matrix with ones on its anti-diagonal and zeros elsewhere and
{\mathbf{A}}
is given by

{\mathbf{A}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathbf{I}}_{\frac{n}{2}}&0\\
0&-{\mathbf{I}}_{\frac{n}{2}}\end{array}\right],

(28)

where
{\mathbf{I}}_{n/2}
is a
n/2\times n/2
identity matrix.

(c)

Set
{\mathbfcal{W}}={\mathbfcal{W}}^{\prime}
.

4:

Output:

{\mathbfcal{W}}
.

We construct the randomized polynomial-time reduction from
{\rm{HPC}}
to
{\rm{ROHC}}
in Algorithm
8
.

Algorithm 8

Randomized Polynomial-time Reduction from HPC to
{\rm{ROHC}}

1:

Input:
Hypergraph
G\sim\mathcal{G}_{d}(n)
, number of iterations
\ell
.

2:

Let
{\rm{RK}}_{G}={\rm{RK}}(1\to N(\xi,1),\frac{1}{2}\to N(0,1),T)
where
T=\lceil 2(d+1)\log_{2}n\rceil
and
\xi=\frac{\log 2}{2\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n+2\log 2}}
and compute the symmetric tensor
{\mathbfcal{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes d}}
with
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}={\rm{RK}}_{G}(\mathbf{1}((i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in E(G)))
. Let the diagonal entries of
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{[i,\ldots,i]}
to be i.i.d.
N(0,1)
.

3:

Generate
(d!-1)
i.i.d. symmetric random tensor
{\mathbfcal{B}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{B}}^{(d!-1)}
in the following way: their diagonal values are
0
and non-diagonal values are i.i.d.
N(0,1)
. Given any non-diagonal index
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})
(
i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\ldots\leq i_{d}
), suppose it has
D\,(D\leq d!)
unique permutations and denote them as
\mathbf{i}_{(0)}:=\mathbf{i},\mathbf{i}_{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{i}_{(D-1)}
, then we transform
{\mathbfcal{W}}
in the following way

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbfcal{W}}_{\mathbf{i}_{(0)}}\\
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{\mathbf{i}_{(1)}}\\
\vdots\\
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{\mathbf{i}_{(D-1)}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}}{\sqrt{d!}},\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}}{\sqrt{d!}}\right)_{\perp}\right)_{[1:D,:]}\times\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbfcal{W}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\\
{\mathbfcal{B}}^{(1)}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\\
\vdots\\
{\mathbfcal{B}}^{(d!-1)}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\end{array}\right).

Here
\frac{\mathbf{1}}{\sqrt{d!}}
is a
\mathbb{R}^{d!}
vector with all entries to be
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d!}}
and
\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}}{\sqrt{d!}}\right)_{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{d!\times(d!-1)}
is an orthogonal complement of
\frac{\mathbf{1}}{\sqrt{d!}}
.

4:

Generate independent permutations
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{d-1}
of
[n]
uniformly at random and let
{\mathbfcal{W}}={\mathbfcal{W}}^{{\rm{id}},\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{d-1}}
.

5:

Apply Tensor Reflecting Cloning to
{\mathbfcal{W}}
with
\ell
iterations.

6:

Output:

{\mathbfcal{W}}
.

The next lemma shows that the randomized polynomial-time mapping we construct in Algorithm
8
maps
{\rm{HPC}}
to
{\rm{ROHC}}
asymptotically.

Lemma 2

Suppose that
n
is even and sufficiently large and
2^{\ell}\kappa<n/C
for some large
C>1
. Let
\xi=\frac{\log 2}{2\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n+2\log 2}}
. Then the randomized polynomial-time map
\varphi:\mathcal{G}_{d}(n)\to\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes d}}
in Algorithm
8
satisfies if
G\sim\mathcal{G}_{d}(n,\frac{1}{2})
, it holds that

{\rm{TV}}\left(\mathcal{L}(\varphi(G)),N(0,1)^{\otimes(n^{\otimes d})}\right)=O(1/n),

and if
G\sim\mathcal{G}_{d}(n,\frac{1}{2},\kappa)
, there is a prior
\pi
on unit vectors in
\mathcal{V}_{n,2^{\ell}\kappa}
such that

{\rm{TV}}\left(\mathcal{L}(\varphi(G)),\int\mathcal{L}\left(\frac{\xi\kappa^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\sqrt{d!}}\mathbf{u}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{u}_{d}+N(0,1)^{\otimes(n^{\otimes d})}\right)d\pi(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\right)=O(1/\sqrt{\log n}).

Here
{\rm{TV}}
denotes the total variation distance and
\mathcal{L}(X)
denotes the distribution of random variable
X
.

Lemma
2
specifically implies that if
k=2^{\ell}\kappa,\mu=\frac{\xi\kappa^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\sqrt{d!}}
with
\xi=\frac{\log 2}{2\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n+2\log 2}}
, the reduction map
\varphi(G)
we constructed from Algorithm
8
satisfies
{\rm{TV}}(\varphi({\rm{HPC}}_{D}(n,\frac{1}{2},\kappa)),{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{k},\mu))\to 0
under both
H_{0}
and
H_{1}
.

Next, we prove the computational lower bound of
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}
under the asymptotic regime (
A2
) by a contradiction argument.

•

If
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2}
(
\alpha
is defined in (
A2
)), i.e., in the dense cluster case, let
\ell=\lceil\frac{2}{d}\beta\log_{2}n\rceil
and
\varphi
be this mapping from Algorithm
8
. Suppose
\kappa=\lceil n^{\gamma}\rceil
in
{\rm{HPC}}_{D}(n,\frac{1}{2},\kappa)
, then after mapping, the sparsity and signal strength in (
A2
) of
{\rm{ROHC}}(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{k},\mu)
model satisfies

\begin{split}&\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log(\mu/k^{\frac{d}{2}})^{-1}}{\log n}=\frac{\frac{d}{2}(\frac{2}{d}\beta+\gamma)\log n-\frac{d}{2}\gamma\log n}{\log n}=\beta,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log k}{\log n}=\frac{2}{d}\beta+\gamma=:\alpha.\end{split}

If
\beta>(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{d}{2}
, there exists a sequence of polynomial-time tests
\{\phi_{n}\}
such that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}}(\phi_{n})<\frac{1}{2}
. Then by Lemmas
2
and 11 in
Luo and Zhang 2020b
Section C, we have
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{HPC}}_{D}}(\phi_{n}\circ\varphi)<\frac{1}{2}
, i.e.
\phi_{n}\circ\varphi
has asymptotic risk less than to
\frac{1}{2}
in HPC detection. On the other hand, the size of the planted clique in HPC satisfies
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log\kappa}{\log n}=\gamma=\alpha-\frac{2}{d}\beta<\alpha-(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})=\frac{1}{2}
. The combination of these two facts contradicts HPC detection conjecture
1
, so we conclude there are no polynomial-time tests
\{\phi_{n}\}
that make
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}}(\phi_{n})<\frac{1}{2}
if
\beta>(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{d}{2}
.

•

If
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
, i.e., in the sparse cluster case, since
{\rm{CHC}}_{D}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda)
is a special case of
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\mu)
with
\mu=\lambda k^{d/2}
, the computational lower bound in
{\rm{CHC}}_{D}
in Theorem
15
implies that if
\beta>0
, then
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}}(\phi_{n})\geq\frac{1}{2}
based on HPC conjecture
1
.

In summary, we conclude if
\beta>(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{d}{2}\vee 0:=\beta^{c}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}}
, any sequence of polynomial-time tests has asymptotic risk at least
1/2
for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{k},\mu)
. This has finished the proof of computational lower bound for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}
.

Next we show the computational lower bound for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
. Suppose there is a sequence of polynomial-time recovery algorithm
\{\phi_{R}\}_{n}
such that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}}(\phi_{R})<\frac{1}{2}
when
\beta>(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{d}{2}\lor 0
. In this regime, it is easy to verify
\mu\geq Ck^{\frac{d}{4}}
for some
C>0
in
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{k},\mu)
. By Lemma 10 in
Luo and Zhang 2020b
Section B, we know there is a sequence polynomial-time detection algorithms
\{\phi_{D}\}_{n}
such that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}}(\phi_{D})<\frac{1}{2}
, which contradicts the computational lower bound established in the first part. This has finished the proof of the computational lower bound for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
.

6
Discussion and Future Work

In this paper, we study the statistical and computational limits of tensor clustering with planted structures, including the constant high-order structure (CHC) and rank-one high-order structure (ROHC). We derive tight statistical lower bounds and tight computational lower bounds under the HPC/HPDS conjectures for both high-order cluster detection and recovery problems. For each problem, we also provide unconstrained-time algorithms and polynomial-time algorithms that respectively achieve these statistical and computational limits. The main results of this paper are summarized in the phase transition diagrams in Figure
1
and Table
1
.

There are a few directions worth exploring in the future. First, this paper mainly focuses on the full high-order clustering in the sense that the signal tensor is sparse along all modes. In practice, the partial cluster also commonly appears (e.g., tensor biclustering
(
Feizi, Javadi and Tse 2017
)
), where the signal is sparse only in part of the modes. It is interesting to investigate the statistical and computational limits for high-order partial clustering. Second, in addition to the exact recovery discussed in this paper, we think our results can be extended to other variants of recovery, such as partial recovery and weak recovery. Third, in the ROHC model, the non-zero components of the signal are required to have the similar magnitudes as this assumption is essential for support recovery. Another interesting problem is to
estimate
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{d})
without the constraint on the component magnitudes of the signal
, which can be seen a rank-one case of the sparse tensor SVD/PCA problem
(
Zhang and
Han 2019
;
Sun et al. 2017
;
Niles-Weed and Zadik 2020
)
. For this problem, the signal-to-noise ratio lower bounds we established in Theorems
10
and
17
still hold by virtue of the estimation-to-detection reduction. However, the ROHC
R
Search and Power-iteration algorithms studied in this paper may no longer be suitable for estimating
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{d})
. A natural unconstrained-time estimator is the maximum likelihood estimator, while to our best knowledge its guarantee is unexplored.
Zhang and
Han 2019
developed efficient algorithms which can achieve the minimax optimal error rate in sparse tensor estimation. However, it is unclear if the required signal-to-noise in
Zhang and
Han 2019
is tight. It is interesting to develop algorithms with optimal guarantees for sparse tensor SVD/PCA under the tight signal-to-noise ratio requirement. Finally, since our computational lower bounds of
{\rm{CHC}}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}
are based on HPC conjecture (Conjecture
1
) and HPDS conjecture (Conjecture
2
), it is interesting to provide more evidence for these conjectures.

A
Proofs of Statistical Bounds for
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}

We begin by introducing a few notations that will be used throughout the proof sections. Given
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{d})
, let
\breve{\mathbf{k}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}
, and
\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-j)}=\prod_{i=1,i\neq j}^{d}k_{i}
. For a random variable
{\mathbf{X}}
, we use notation
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{X}})
to denote the distribution of
{\mathbf{X}}
. Given a tensor
{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
,
\mathcal{L}({\mathbfcal{X}}+N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}})
denotes the distribution of
{\mathbfcal{X}}+{\mathbfcal{Z}}
where
{\mathbfcal{Z}}
is drawn from
N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
.

A.1
Proof of Theorem
3

Since
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda)
is a special case of
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}})
, the statistical lower bound for
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
implies the lower bound for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
. So we only need to show the statistical lower bound for
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
. Also to show the statistical lower bound of
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
, it is enough to show for the case
{\mathbfcal{X}}=\lambda\mathbf{1}_{I_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{1}_{I_{d}}
.

Note that for any
\{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}\}\subseteq\mathscr{X}_{{\rm{CHC}}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda)
,

\inf_{\phi_{R}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{R}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathscr{X}_{{\rm{CHC}}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda)}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\phi_{R}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\neq S({\mathbfcal{X}}))\geq\inf_{\phi_{R}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{R}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}\}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\phi_{R}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\neq S({\mathbfcal{X}})).

Next, we aim to select an appropriate set
\{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}\}
and give a lower bound for

\inf_{\phi_{R}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{R}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}\}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\phi_{R}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\neq S({\mathbfcal{X}})).

We set
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}=\lambda 1_{I^{(0)}_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ 1_{I^{(0)}_{d}}
, where
I^{(0)}_{i}=[k_{i}]
is the signal support of mode
i
.
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}

(M=n_{1}-k_{1})
are constructed in the following way. Assume for mode 2 to mode
d
,
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}
have the same signal support as
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}
, but for
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}
, its signal support on mode 1 is
I^{(i)}_{1}:=[k_{1}-1]\bigcup\{(i+k_{1})\}
.

By such construction,
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}
are close to each other. Now we calculate the KL divergence between
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}}
. Observe that
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}}
are the same except that one of the index of signal support of
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}}
on mode 1 is changed from
\{k_{1}\}
to
\{(i+k_{1})\}
,

\begin{split}{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}},\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}})&=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{KL}}(Z_{\lambda},Z_{0})+\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{KL}}(Z_{0},Z_{\lambda})\\
&=\prod_{j=2}^{d}k_{j}(\lambda^{2}/2)+\prod_{j=2}^{d}k_{j}(\lambda^{2}/2)\\
&=\lambda^{2}\prod_{j=2}^{d}k_{j},\end{split}

(29)

where
Z_{\lambda}\sim N(\lambda,1)
and
Z_{0}\sim N(0,1)
. So if
\lambda\leq\sqrt{\frac{\eta\log(n_{1}-k_{1})}{\prod_{j=2}^{d}k_{j}}}
, then
{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}},\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}})\leq\eta\log(n_{1}-k_{1})
.
So,

\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{i}},\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0}})\leq\eta\log M.

(30)

By Theorem 2.5 of
Tsybakov 2009
, for
0<\eta<1/8
, we have

\inf_{\phi_{R}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{R}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{X}}_{M}\}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\phi_{R}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\neq S({\mathbfcal{X}}))\geq\frac{\sqrt{M}}{1+\sqrt{M}}\left(1-2\eta-\frac{2\eta}{\log M}\right)\to 1-2\eta,

(31)

where the limit is taken under the asymptotic regime (
A1
).

Notice that in the above construction, we only change the signal support in mode 1. Similarly, we could construct a parameter set which only differs at the signal support at mode
j
,
2\leq j\leq d
. By repeating the argument above, we have if

\lambda\leq\max\left(\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\eta\log(n_{i}-k_{i})}{\prod_{j=1,j\neq i}^{d}k_{j}}}\right\}_{i=1}^{d}\right),

(32)

then the minimax estimation error converges to
1-2\eta
.

Finally, the second part of the conclusion follows by considering the asymptotic regime (
A2
). For
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
, we have

\begin{split}\beta>(d-1)\alpha/2=:\beta^{s}_{{\rm{CHC}}_{R}}\Longrightarrow n^{-\beta}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{\alpha(d-1)}}}\overset{\eqref{eq: comp lower bound asymptotic regime}}{\Longrightarrow}\lambda\leq\max\left(\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\eta\log(n_{i}-k_{i})}{\prod_{z=1,z\neq i}^{d}k_{z}}}\right\}_{i=1}^{d}\right).\end{split}

Similar derivation can be done for ROHC
R
as well and this has finished the proof.

A.2
Proof of Theorem
4

Denote
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})
. Given any signal support set
(I_{1},\ldots,I_{d})
, define
F(I_{1},\ldots,I_{d})=\sum_{i_{1}\in I_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}\in I_{d}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}
. Since the problem only becomes easier as
\lambda
increases, it is enough to show the algorithm succeeds in the hardest case
{\mathbfcal{X}}=\lambda\cdot 1_{I_{1}}\circ\cdots 1_{I_{d}}
.

Suppose the true signal support of
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\sim\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
is
(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*})
. By construction, the output of Algorithm
1
satisfies

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}((\hat{I}_{1},\ldots,\hat{I}_{d})\neq(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*}))&=\mathbb{P}\left(\underset{(\tilde{I}_{1},\ldots\tilde{I}_{d})\neq(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*})}{\bigcup}\left\{F(\tilde{I}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{I}_{d})>F(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*})\right\}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=0}^{k_{d}}P_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}-P_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}},\end{split}

(33)

where

\begin{split}P_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}&=\mathbb{P}\left(F(\tilde{I}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{I}_{d})>F(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*})\right)\\
&=\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}\binom{k_{j}}{i_{j}}\binom{n_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}-i_{j}}\right)\bar{\Phi}\left(\lambda\sqrt{(\breve{\mathbf{k}}-\breve{\mathbf{i}}})/2\right),\end{split}

here
\bar{\Phi}(\cdot)
is the survival function of c.d.f. of a standard Gaussian distribution and
i_{j}=|\tilde{I}_{j}\bigcap I_{j}^{*}|\,(1\leq j\leq d)
.

To bound the right hand side of (
33
), we first decompose
\left(\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=0}^{k_{d}}P_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}\right)
into
(d+1)
different groups. Specifically:

•

In group 0,
i_{j}\leq k_{j}-1
for all
1\leq j\leq d
, we denote the summation of terms in this group as
T_{0}
.

•

In group 1, exists one
j^{*}\in[d]
such that
i_{j^{*}}=k_{j^{*}}
and for
j\neq j^{*}
,
i_{j}\leq k_{j}-1
. We denote the summation of terms in this group as
T_{1}
.

•

In group 2, there exists two distinct indices
j_{1}^{*},j_{2}^{*}\in[d]
such that
i_{j_{1}^{*}}=k_{j_{1}^{*}}
and
i_{j_{2}^{*}}=k_{j_{2}^{*}}
. For the rest of indices
j\neq j_{1}^{*},j_{2}^{*}
,
i_{j}\leq k_{j}-1
. We denote the summation of terms in this group as
T_{2}
.

•

Similarly we can define for group
j\,(3\leq j\leq d-1)
. Denote the summation of terms in group
j
as
T_{j}
.

•

In group d, there is only one term
P_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}}
.

First notice that the term in group
d
cancels with the
\left(-P_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}}\right)
in (
33
). Next we are going to give an upper bound for
T_{0},T_{1},\ldots,T_{d-1}
. Since the strategy to bound each of them is similar, we only need to demonstrate how to bound
T_{0}
.

\begin{split}T_{0}&\leq\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(k_{j}-1)\right)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{z}=0,\ldots,k_{z}-1\\
z=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}{\max}P_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\\
&\leq\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(k_{j}-1)\right)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{z}=0,\ldots,k_{z}-1\\
z=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}{\max}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(n_{j}-k_{j})^{2(k_{j}-i_{j})}\right)\bar{\Phi}\left(\lambda\sqrt{(\breve{\mathbf{k}}-\breve{\mathbf{i}}})/2\right)\\
&\leq\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(k_{j}-1)\right)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{z}=0,\ldots,k_{z}-1\\
z=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}{\max}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(n_{j}-k_{j})^{2(k_{j}-i_{j})}\right){\rm{exp}}\,\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\left(\breve{\mathbf{k}}-\breve{\mathbf{i}}\right)\right)\\
&\leq\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{z}=0,\ldots,k_{z}-1\\
z=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}{\max}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(n_{j}-k_{j})^{3(k_{j}-i_{j})}\right){\rm{exp}}\,\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\left(\breve{\mathbf{k}}-\frac{1}{d}\sum_{z=1}^{d}i_{z}\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-z)}\right)\right),\end{split}

(34)

where the second inequality is the result of plugging in
P_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}
and the fact
{k_{j}\choose i_{j}}{n_{j}-k_{j}\choose k_{j}-i_{j}}\leq(n_{j}-k_{j})^{2(k_{j}-i_{j})}
when
k_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2}n_{i}
, the third inequality is by concentration bound for Gaussian random variable
\mathbb{P}(Z_{0}>t)\leq\frac{1}{t}{\rm{exp}}(-t^{2}/2)
and the last inequality is due to
k_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2}n_{i}
and
\frac{1}{d}\sum_{z=1}^{d}i_{z}\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-z)}\geq\breve{\mathbf{i}}
.

The maximum value of right hand side of (
34
) is achieved when
i_{1}=k_{1}-1,\ldots,i_{d}=k_{d}-1
and we have

T_{0}\leq\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}(n_{j}-k_{j})^{3}\right){\rm{exp}}\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4d}\sum_{z=1}^{d}\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-z)}\right).

(35)

So when

\lambda^{2}\geq C\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\log(n_{i}-k_{i})}{\min_{1\leq i\leq d}\{\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-i)}\}},

for large enough constant
C
(only depend on
d
), we have

T_{0}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}(n_{i}-k_{i})^{-c},

for some constant
c>0
.

Similar analysis holds for
T_{j}
(
1\leq j\leq d-1
). This has finished the proof.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
5

We first introduce a few notations and the rest of the proof is divided into three steps. Suppose
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\sim\mathcal{L}(\mu\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{v}_{d}+N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}})
and
\mathbf{v}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{i},k_{i}}
. First it is easy to check that
{\mathbfcal{A}},{\mathbfcal{B}}
are independent and have the same distribution
\mathcal{L}(\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{v}_{d}+N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}})
. Denote
k_{i}^{*}=|\mathbf{v}_{i}|
,
\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}=\mathbf{1}_{S(\mathbf{v}_{i})}
. Since
(\mathbf{v}_{i})_{j}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{k_{i}}}
, the number of non-zero entries in
\mathbf{v}_{i}
is at least
ck_{i}
for some small
c>0
.

Step 1
. In this step, we show with high probability the marked pairs in Step 3 of Algorithm
2
are supported on
(S(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\ldots,S(\mathbf{v}_{d}))
. First for
(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\in S_{\bar{k}_{1}}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{\bar{k}_{d}}^{n_{d}}
, if one of
\mathbf{u}_{i}
is not supported on corresponding
S(\mathbf{v}_{i})
, we show when
\mu\geq C\sqrt{k\log n}
, such
(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})
will not be marked in Algorithm
2
Step 3(b) with probability at least
1-n^{-(d+1)}
.

Without loss of generality, suppose
S(\mathbf{u}_{1})\nsubseteq S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
and let
j\in S(\mathbf{u}_{1})\setminus S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
. Notice

\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1})_{j}\sim N(0,\prod_{i=2}^{d}\bar{k}_{i}).

Then by the Gaussian tail bounds, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1})_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\bar{k}_{i}\right)\\
\leq&{\rm{exp}}\left(-\frac{\mu^{2}(\prod_{i=2}^{d}\bar{k}_{i})^{2}}{16\prod_{i=2}^{d}\bar{k}_{i}\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}\right)\\
\leq&{\rm{exp}}(-c\frac{\mu^{2}}{k_{1}})\leq n^{-(d+1)},\end{split}

(36)

where the last inequality holds because
\mu\geq C\sqrt{k\log n}
for sufficient large
C>0
. Similar analysis holds for other modes.

If
(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})
is marked, let
(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{d}})=(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})
, otherwise let
(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{d}})=(0,\ldots,0)
. Since
{\mathbfcal{B}}
is independent of
{\mathbfcal{A}}
, applying a union bound, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(S(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{1}})\subseteq S(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\ldots,S(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{d}})\subseteq S(\mathbf{v}_{d})\text{ for all }\bar{k}_{i}\in[1,k_{i}],1\leq i\leq d\right)\\
\geq&1-\sum_{\bar{k}_{i}\in[1,k_{i}],1\leq i\leq d}\mathbb{P}\left(S(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{i}})\nsubseteq S(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\\
\geq&1-(\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i})n^{-(d+1)}\geq 1-n^{-1}.\end{split}

(37)

Step 2
. Let

(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d})=\arg\max_{(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\in S_{k_{1}^{*}}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{d}^{*}}^{n_{d}}}{\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\ldots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}.

(38)

In this step, we show that
(S(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}),\ldots,S(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}))=(S(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\ldots,S(\mathbf{v}_{d}))
with high probability. Let
{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}\times n_{i}}\,(1\leq i\leq d)
be a diagonal matrix with its diagonal values

({\mathbf{A}}_{i})_{jj}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\text{ if }(\mathbf{v}_{i})_{j}=0\\
1&\text{ if }(\mathbf{v}_{i})_{j}>0\\
-1&\text{ if }(\mathbf{v}_{i})_{j}<0.\end{array}\right.

So
{\mathbfcal{A}}
can be rewritten as
\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{2}\mathbf{v}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{A}}_{d}^{2}\mathbf{v}_{d}+{\mathbfcal{Z}}
where
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\sim N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
. So

\begin{split}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d})&=\arg\max_{(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\in S_{k_{1}^{*}}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{d}^{*}}^{n_{d}}}{\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\ldots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}\\
&=\arg\max_{(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\in S_{k_{1}^{*}}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{d}^{*}}^{n_{d}}}\left(\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot{\mathbf{A}}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{A}}_{d}\mathbf{v}_{d}\right)\times_{1}({\mathbf{A}}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{1})^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}({\mathbf{A}}_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d})^{\top}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}.\end{split}

(39)

Notice that in optimization problem (
39
),
{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{i}
has positive values at its support and these positive values have magnitude at least
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k_{i}}}
. Also since the diagonal entries of
{\mathbf{A}}_{i}
capture the exact support of
\mathbf{v}_{i}
, problem in (
39
) is a modified version of constant high-order clustering problem with
\lambda=\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}
and by a similar argument of Theorem
4
,
(S(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}),\ldots,S(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}))=(S(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\ldots,S(\mathbf{v}_{d}))
and sign
(\mathbf{u}_{i})=
sign
(\mathbf{v}_{i})
with probability at least
1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}(n_{i}-k_{i})^{-1}
when
\lambda=\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log(n_{j}-k_{j})}{\underset{1\leq i\leq d}{\min}\{\prod_{z=1,z\neq i}^{d}k_{z}\}}}
i.e.,
\mu\geq C\sqrt{k\log n}
for some
C>0
.

Step 3
. In this last step, we show
(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*})
will be marked with high probability. Consider the analysis for mode-1 first. If
j\in S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
,

\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}\sim N\left(\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1})_{j}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n_{2}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{n_{d}}(\mathbf{v}_{2})_{[i_{2}]}\ldots(\mathbf{v}_{d})_{[i_{d}]},\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}^{*}\right),

notice

\mu_{1}:=\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1})_{j}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n_{2}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{n_{d}}(\mathbf{v}_{2})_{[i_{2}]}\ldots(\mathbf{v}_{d})_{[i_{d}]}\geq\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}k^{*}_{i}.

By Gaussian tail bound, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}^{*}\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}-\mu_{1}\leq-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}^{*}\right)\leq n^{-(d+1)},\end{split}

where the last inequality is obtained in the same way as in (
36
). Similarly, if
j\notin S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
, then

\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}\sim N(0,\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}^{*}),

and by the same argument of (
36
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}^{*}\right)\leq n^{-(d+1)}.

By a union bound, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(S(\mathbf{v}_{1})=\left\{j:\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{1})_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}k^{*}_{i}\right\}\right)\\
\geq&1-\sum_{j\notin S(\mathbf{v}_{i})}\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*})_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}k_{i}^{*}\right)\\
&-\sum_{j\in S(\mathbf{v}_{i})}\mathbb{P}\left(\left({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{*\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*\top}\right)_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{*}_{1})_{j}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}}}\prod_{i=2}k_{i}^{*}\right)\\
\geq&1-(n_{1}-k_{1})n^{-(d+1)}-k_{1}n^{-(d+1)}.\end{split}

Similar analysis holds for other modes, so a union bound yields that
(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*})
is marked in Step (3b) with probability at least
1-n^{-d}
.

Summarize the result so far, with probability at least
1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}(n_{i}-k_{i})^{-1}-n^{-d}
, we have

•

S(\mathbf{u}_{\bar{k}_{i}})\subseteq S(\mathbf{v}_{i})
for all
\bar{k}_{i}\in[1,k_{i}]
and all
1\leq i\leq d
.

•

(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\mathbf{u}^{*}_{d})=\arg\max_{(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d})\in S_{k_{1}^{*}}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{d}^{*}}^{n_{d}}}{\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{u}_{d}^{\top}.

•

(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{d}^{*})
is marked in Step (3b).

With these three points, we conclude with probably at least
1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}(n_{i}-k_{i})^{-1}-n^{-d}
, the algorithm can output the true support.

A.4
Proof of Theorem
6

The idea to prove this theorem is to use the Gaussian tail bound
\mathbb{P}(Z_{0}\geq t)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{t}{\rm{exp}}(-\frac{t^{2}}{2})
, where
Z_{0}\sim N(0,1)
. Suppose
I_{i}=S(\mathbf{v}_{i})\,(1\leq i\leq d)
. If
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\notin I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}
, then
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\sim N(0,1)
, so

\mathbb{P}\left(|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}|\geq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)=2\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{0}\geq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}}n^{-(d+1)}=O(n^{-(d+1)}).

For
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}
, then
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\sim N(\lambda,1)
with
\lambda\geq 2\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}
for
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
and
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}\sim N(\mu\cdot(\mathbf{v}_{1})_{i_{1}}\ldots(\mathbf{v}_{d})_{i_{d}},1)
for
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
. Since
(\mathbf{v}_{j})_{i_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k_{j}}}
by assumption
\mathbf{v}_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{j},k_{j}}
,
\mu\cdot(\mathbf{v}_{1})_{i_{1}}\ldots(\mathbf{v}_{d})_{i_{d}}\geq 2\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}
. This implies for both
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}|\leq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{0}\leq-\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)\leq O(n^{-(d+1)}).

So the probability that the set
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})
with
|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}|\geq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}
is not exactly
I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}
is, by union bound, at most

\begin{split}&\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\notin I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}}\mathbb{P}\left(|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}|\geq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)\\
+&\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}}\mathbb{P}\left(|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}|\leq\sqrt{2(d+1)\log n}\right)=O(n^{-1}),\end{split}

which completes the proof of this theorem.

A.5
Proof of Theorem
7

For any tensor
{\mathbfcal{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
,denote
{\mathbfcal{W}}_{k}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{k-1}\times n_{k+1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
as the subtensor of
{\mathbfcal{W}}
by fixing the index of
k^{th}
mode of
{\mathbfcal{W}}
to be
i
and range over all indices among other modes.

First, in Algorithm
4
, we observe in both models, we have
{\mathbfcal{A}}\sim{\mathbfcal{X}}+\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{Z}}}
and
{\mathbfcal{B}}\sim{\mathbfcal{X}}+{\mathbfcal{Z}}
, where
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{Z}}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}
are independent random variable with distribution
N(0,1)^{\otimes n_{1}\times\cdots\times n_{d}}
. In CHC model,
{\mathbfcal{X}}=\lambda/\sqrt{2}\cdot 1_{I_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ 1_{I_{d}}
; in ROHC model,
{\mathbfcal{X}}=\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{v}_{d}
.

By the proof of Theorem 1 of
Zhang and Xia 2018
, when

\lambda\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}\geq n^{\frac{d}{4}}\quad\text{ or }\quad\mu\geq Cn^{\frac{d}{4}},

(40)

where
\breve{\mathbf{k}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}
, then w.p. at least
1-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
, we have

\left\|P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{i}}-P_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\right\|\leq 2\left\|\sin\Theta(\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{i},\mathbf{u}_{i})\right\|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{n_{i}}}{\lambda\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}}\text{ or }C\frac{\sqrt{n_{i}}}{\mu}\quad\text{ for }1\leq i\leq d,

(41)

here
P_{\mathbf{u}}=\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}
denotes the projection operator onto the subspace expanded by
\mathbf{u}
and
\|\cdot\|
is the spectral norm of a matrix. Next, we consider
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}
separately.

For
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{k},\lambda)
. We consider the analysis for mode 1 signal support recovery. First

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
=&\left\|({\mathbfcal{B}}-{\mathbfcal{X}})_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
\leq&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}+\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
\leq&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}+\sum_{k=2}^{d}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\left\|P_{\mathbf{u}_{k}}-P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}}\right\|,\end{split}

(42)

here
\|{\mathbfcal{A}}\|_{{\rm{HS}}}=\left(\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}^{2}\right)^{1/2}
is the Hilbert-Schmidt norm for tensor
{\mathbfcal{A}}
. The last inequality is due to triangle inequality and the following decomposition of
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}
,

\begin{split}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}=&{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\\
&+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d-1}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d-1}}\times_{d}({\mathbf{I}}_{n_{d}}-P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}})\\
&+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d-2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d-2}}\times_{d-1}({\mathbf{I}}_{n_{d-1}}-P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d-1}})\\
&+\ldots+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}({\mathbf{I}}_{n_{2}}-P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}})\\
=&{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}+\sum_{i=2}^{d}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\times_{1<j<i}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}}\times_{i}\left(P_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}-P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{i}}\right).\end{split}

Since
{\mathbfcal{A}}
and
{\mathbfcal{B}}
are independent,
{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}
and
P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}},\ldots,P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}
are independent. So we have

\left\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}=\left|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\right|>\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})},

with probability at most
n_{1}^{-(c+1)}
. By union bound, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,n_{1}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\geq\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}\right)\leq n_{1}^{-c}.

(43)

Combining (
43
) with (
41
), we get an upper bound for (
42
),

\begin{split}\max_{i}\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}&\leq\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}},\end{split}

(44)

with probability at least
1-n_{1}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
. Here we use the fact
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\leq\lambda\sqrt{\prod_{i=2}^{d}k_{i}}
.

Then for
i\in I_{1},i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}
, condition on (
44
), we have

\begin{split}&\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\\
=&\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
=&\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}+{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
\geq&-\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}+\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}-\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
\geq&\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-1)}}-2\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right),\end{split}

(45)

here
\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{-1}=\prod_{i}k_{i}/k_{1}
.
Similarly, for
i\in I_{1},i^{\prime}\in I_{1}
(or
i,i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}
),

\begin{split}&\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\\
\leq&\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}+\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}+\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\\
\leq&2\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(46)

So when

\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-1)}}\geq 6\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right),

we have

\begin{split}&2\underset{i,i^{\prime}\in I_{1}\text{ or }i,i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}}{\max}\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\\
&\leq\underset{i\in I_{1},i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}}{\min}\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|.\end{split}

So a simple cutoff at the maximum gap at the ordered value of

\left\{{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right\}_{i=1}^{n_{1}}

can identify the right support of mode 1 with probability at least
1-n_{1}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
.

Similar analysis holds for mode
2,3,\ldots,d
and combined with SNR requirement in the initialization (
40
), when

\lambda\geq C\frac{n^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}}\lor 6\left(\frac{\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n)}}{\min_{i=1,\ldots,d}\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}^{(-i)}}}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}}\right),

then with probability at least
1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}(n_{i}^{-c}+C{\rm{exp}}(-cn))
, we have
\hat{I}_{i}=I_{i}^{*},(1\leq i\leq d)
. Notice the initialization SNR requirement will be the dominate one when
d\geq 3
, so the final SNR requirement for
\lambda
is
\lambda\geq C\frac{n^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}}
.

Next consider
{\rm{ROHC}}_{R}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\mu)
. The proof of this part is similar to the
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
part.

First, when
\mu\geq Cn^{d/4}
, then w.p. at least
1-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
, we have (
41
). The rest of the proof is the same as the first part
{\rm{CHC}}_{R}
proof after equation (
41
) except that in (
45
) and (
46
), we need to consider separately of the support index with positive values and negative values.

Suppose
I_{1}=I_{1+}\bigcup I_{1-}
where
I_{1+}
denotes the indices in
S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
that have positive values in
\mathbf{v}_{1}
and
I_{1-}
denotes the indices in
S(\mathbf{v}_{1})
that have negative values in
\mathbf{v}_{1}
.

By the same argument as (
44
) and the fact
\mathbf{v}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{i},k_{i}}
, with probability at least
1-n_{1}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
, we have

\max_{i}\left\|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}}\times\cdots\times_{d}P_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{{\rm{HS}}}\leq\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}.

(47)

Similarly, if
i\in I_{1},i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}
, condition on (
47
), we have

\begin{split}&\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\geq\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2k_{1}}}-2\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(48)

For
i\in I_{1+}
(
i\in I_{1-}
or
i\notin I_{1}
) and
i^{\prime}\in I_{1+}
(
i\in I_{1-}
or
i\notin I_{1}
),

\begin{split}&\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\leq 2\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(49)

For
i\in I_{1+}
and
i\in I_{1-}
,

\begin{split}&\left|{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}-{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i^{\prime}}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right|\geq 2\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2k_{1}}}-2\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(50)

So when

\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2k_{1}}}\geq 6\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n_{1})}+C(d-1)\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{k_{1}}}\right),

a simple two cuts at the top two maximum gaps at the ordered values of

\left\{{\mathbfcal{B}}_{1}^{i}\times_{2}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{\top}\times\cdots\times_{d}\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{d}^{\top}\right\}_{i=1}^{n_{1}}

can identify the right support of
I_{1+},I_{1-}
and
[n_{1}]\setminus I_{1}
with probability at least
1-n_{1}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
. Since
|I_{1+}|,|I_{1+}|\leq k_{1}\ll n_{1}
, pick two small clusters could yield the right support. Similar analysis holds for other modes, combining with the SNR requirement in initialization (
40
), when

\mu\geq Cn^{\frac{d}{4}}\lor 6\left(\sqrt{2(c+1)\log(n)k}+C(d-1)\sqrt{n}\right),

with probability at least
1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}n_{i}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn)
, we have
\hat{I}_{i}=I_{i}^{*},(1\leq i\leq d)
. Notice the initialization SNR requirement will be the dominate one when
d\geq 3
, the final SNR requirement for
\mu
is
\mu\geq Cn^{\frac{d}{4}}
. This has finished the proof.

A.6
Proof of Theorem
8

Assume
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\mathbfcal{X}}
from model (
1
) and the true support
S({\mathbfcal{X}})=(I_{1}^{*},\ldots,I_{d}^{*})
. We first take a look at mode 1 signal support recovery. Recall
1^{*}
is the index such that
1^{*}=\arg\min_{j\neq 1}n_{j}
and

{\mathbf{Y}}_{[k_{1},k_{2}]}^{(1,1^{*})}:=\frac{{\rm{SUM}}({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{(1,1^{*})})}{\sqrt{\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}n_{j}}}\quad\text{ for }\quad 1\leq k_{1}\leq n_{1},1\leq k_{2}\leq n_{1^{*}}.

(51)

It is not hard to check that if
{\mathbfcal{Y}}=\lambda\cdot\mathbf{1}_{I_{1}^{*}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{1}_{I_{d}^{*}}+{\mathbfcal{Z}}
, then

{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1,1^{*})}=\frac{\lambda\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}k_{j}}{\sqrt{\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}n_{j}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{1}^{*}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{I_{1^{*}}^{*}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{Z}},

where
{\mathbf{Z}}
has i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries.

Now the problem reduces to the submatrix localization problem studied in literature given parameter

(n_{1},n_{1^{*}},k_{1},k_{1^{*}},\frac{\lambda\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}k_{j}}{\sqrt{\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}^{d}n_{j}}}).

By Lemma 1 of
Cai, Liang and
Rakhlin 2017
, if

\frac{\lambda\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}k_{j}}{\sqrt{\prod_{j\neq 1,1^{*}}n_{j}}}\sqrt{k_{1}^{*}}\geq C\left(\sqrt{\frac{n_{1^{*}}}{k_{1}}}+\sqrt{\log n_{1}}\right),

for a large
C>0
, then w.p. at least
1-n_{1}^{-c}-C{\rm{exp}}(-cn_{1})
, we have
\hat{I}_{1}=I_{1}^{*}
, and here
c,C>0
are some universal constants.

Analysis for other modes are similar and this has finished the proof.

B
Proofs of Statistical bounds for
{\rm{CHC}}_{D}
and
{\rm{ROHC}}_{D}

B.1
Proof of Theorem
9

The proof of Theorem
9
is fairly long and the main idea is to reduce the minimax testing risk to a Bayesian testing risk with uniform prior over the set of parameters. The main technical difficulty is to bound the second moment of the truncated likelihood ratio, see Lemma
5
. For
d=2
, the lower bound for constant matrix clustering detection has been proved in
Butucea and
Ingster 2013
, however it is much more challenging to show it in order-
d
case. In this section, we will first prove the main theorem and its subsections are devoted to prove Lemmas used in Theorem.

First, we define some convenient notations. Given a vector
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{x}}:={n_{1}\choose x_{1}}\cdots{n_{d}\choose x_{d}}
and let
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C}=\frac{\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in C}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}}{\sqrt{k_{1}\cdots k_{d}}}
for any
C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}
, here
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}
is the collection of all possible choices of signal locations in the big tensor defined in (
13
). Given
{\mathbfcal{Y}}
, we use notation
\mathbb{P}_{C}:=\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
where
C=\mathcal{S}({\mathbfcal{X}})
to denote the distribution of a tensor with the high-order cluster supported on
C
. Let
\pi
be a uniform prior on all elements in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}
, i.e.,

\pi=\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\delta_{C},

and
\mathbb{P}_{\pi}
be the mixture of distributions
\mathbb{P}_{\pi}=\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\mathbb{P}_{C}
. Denote the likelihood ratio to be:

\begin{split}{\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}}):=\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{\pi}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=&\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}{\rm{exp}}(-\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}/2+\lambda\sqrt{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C})\\
=&\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}/2+b{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C}).\end{split}

where
\breve{\mathbf{k}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}
and
b^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}
. To show the lower bound, it suffices to show

\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\left|{\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})-1\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\to 0,\quad\forall\epsilon>0.

(52)

Since

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{CHC}}_{D}}^{s}&:=\inf_{\phi_{D}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{D}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{0}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=1)+\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathscr{X}_{{\rm{CHC}}_{D}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n},\lambda)}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbfcal{X}}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=0)\right)\\
&\geq\inf_{\phi_{D}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{D}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{0}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=1)+\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\mathbb{P}_{C}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=0)\right)\\
&=\inf_{\phi_{D}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{D}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{0}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))+\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[(1-\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{C}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}({\mathbfcal{Y}})]\right)\\
&=\inf_{\phi_{D}\in{\rm{AllAlg}}^{D}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{0}(\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))+\mathbb{E}_{0}[(1-\phi_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})){\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})]\right)\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}_{0}(\phi^{*}_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))+\mathbb{E}_{0}[(1-\phi^{*}_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})){\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})],\end{split}

(53)

where
\phi^{*}_{D}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=1({\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})>1)
is the likelihood ratio test, take liminf at both side of (
53
) and by Fatou’s lemma, it is easy to get
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}_{{\rm{CHC}}_{D}}^{s}\to 1
if
{\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\to 1
in
\mathbb{P}_{0}
probability.

One canonical way to show
{\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\to 1
in
\mathbb{P}_{0}
probability is to show
\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{LR}}_{\pi}^{2}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))\to 1
and then use chebyshev’s inequality. However, the direct calculation of
\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{LR}}_{\pi}^{2}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))
does not work here and we replace
{\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})
by its truncated version,

\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=\left(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{C}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}({\mathbfcal{Y}})1_{\Gamma_{C}}.

(54)

Here
\Gamma_{C}
is defined as follows: take small
\delta_{1}>0
(will be specified later) and for
\mathbf{v}=(v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{d})
, let
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},C}=\{V\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}:V\subset C\}
be the sub-support set of
C
which are in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}
and define

\Gamma_{C}=\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\underset{V\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{v},C}}{\bigcap}\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}\leq T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\},

where
T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}=\sqrt{2(\log G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{v}}+\log\prod_{i=1}^{d}k_{i})}
. It is easy to check, under asymptotic regime (
A1
) and
\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}
, we have

T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}\sim 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{v_{i}})\right)\sim 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\right),

(55)

here
a_{n}\sim b_{n}
if
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}=1
.

Now we introduce the first Lemma.

Lemma 3

Set
\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}=\bigcap_{C\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\Gamma_{C}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}})=1
.

This yields
\mathbb{P}_{0}({\rm{LR}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})=\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))\to 1
. So in place of checking (
52
), it is sufficient to check
\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\to 1
in
\mathbb{P}_{0}
probability. To show this, we change it to show the following two Lemmas.

Lemma 4

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi})\to 1
.

Lemma 5

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}^{2})\leq 1+o(1)
.

Lemma
4
and
5
imply that

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}-1)^{2}=(\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}^{2})-1)-2(\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi})-1)\leq o(1).

(56)

This has finished the proof of Theorem
9
.

B.1.1
Proof of Lemma
3

It suffices to check
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}^{c})\to 0
, where
\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}^{c}
is the complement of event
\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}
. First,

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}^{c}=&\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\bigcup_{V\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{v},C}}\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}>T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}\\
=&\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\bigcup_{V\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}}\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}>T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}.\end{split}

(57)

Since
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\sim N(0,1)
under
\mathbb{P}_{0}
, by definition of
T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}
and using the asymptotics
\Phi(-x)\sim e^{-x^{2}/2}/\sqrt{2\pi}x
as
x\to\infty
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{k}}^{c})&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}}\Phi(-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{v}}\Phi(-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\frac{1+o(1)}{\breve{\mathbf{k}}T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\sqrt{2\pi}}\to 0.\end{split}

This has finished the proof.

B.1.2
Proof of Lemma
4

In view of symmetry for
C
in (
54
), it suffices to check that, for any fixed
C\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}
,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left(\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{C}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}1_{\Gamma_{C}}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{C}(\Gamma_{C})\to 1,

or equivalently,
\mathbb{P}_{C}\left(\Gamma_{C}^{c}\right)\to 0
. Since
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\sim N(z_{\mathbf{v}},1)
under the
\mathbb{P}_{C}
, where
z_{\mathbf{v}}^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{v}}:=\lambda^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{\mathbf{v}}_{i}
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{C}(\Gamma_{C}^{c})\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{v},C}}\Phi(z_{\mathbf{v}}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}G_{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathbf{k}}\Phi(z_{\mathbf{v}}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}),

where
G_{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathbf{k}}={k_{1}\choose v_{1}}\cdots{k_{d}\choose v_{d}}
. By the condition (
10
), there exists
\delta>0
,

b^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}<(2-\delta)\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}.

(58)

Let
\delta_{1}
small enough such that when
\delta_{1}k_{1}\leq v_{1}\leq k_{1},\ldots,\delta_{1}k_{d}\leq v_{d}\leq k_{d}
, combining with (
55
) we have,

\begin{split}z_{\mathbf{v}}^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{v}}&<\frac{2-\delta}{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\breve{\mathbf{v}}\leq(2-\delta)\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\\
&\sim(1-\frac{\delta}{2})T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}.\end{split}

Thus there exists
\delta>0
,

\Phi(z_{\mathbf{v}}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})\leq{\rm{exp}}(-\frac{\delta}{2}T^{2}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}).

By Stirling’s formula,
\log\left(G_{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathbf{k}}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\log({k_{i}\choose v_{i}})\sim\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{k_{i}}{v_{i}}=O(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i})
, where the last equality is because
\frac{k_{i}}{v_{i}}\leq\frac{1}{\delta_{1}}
. On the other hand,
T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}\sim\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\gg\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}
under the asymptotic regime (
A1
), so

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}G_{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathbf{k}}\Phi(z_{\mathbf{v}}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1}k_{i}\leq v_{i}\leq k_{i}\\
i=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}{\rm{exp}}(O(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i})-\frac{\delta}{2}T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2})\to 0.

This has finished the proof.

B.1.3
Proof of Lemma
5

First,

\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}^{2})=\left(G_{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-2}\sum_{C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\Gamma_{C_{1}}\cap\Gamma_{C_{2}}}).

Denote two latent supports as
C_{1}=A_{1}\times A_{2}\times\cdots\times A_{d}
and
C_{2}=B_{1}\times B_{2}\times\cdots\times B_{d}
where
A_{i},B_{i}\subseteq[n_{i}]
and
|A_{i}|=|B_{i}|=k_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq d
. Denote the intersection part of
C_{1},C_{2}
as
V
and its dimension as
\mathbf{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})
, i.e.,
V=(A_{1}\bigcap B_{1})\times\cdots\times(A_{d}\bigcap B_{d})
and
v_{1}=|A_{1}\bigcap B_{1}|,\ldots,v_{d}=|A_{d}\bigcap B_{d}|
.

Notice that the value of
\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\Gamma_{C_{1}}\bigcap\Gamma_{C_{2}}})
only depends on the size of
V
. So given
V=C_{1}\bigcap C_{2}
, let

g(\mathbf{v}):=\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\Gamma_{C_{1}}\bigcap\Gamma_{C_{2}}}).

(59)

Then

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\widetilde{{\rm{LR}}}_{\pi}^{2})&=\sum_{v_{1}=0}^{k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=0}^{k_{d}}\frac{\#\left((C_{1},C_{2})\in\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}:\text{ size}(V)=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})\right)}{G_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}}g(\mathbf{v})\\
&=\sum_{v_{1}=0}^{k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=0}^{k_{d}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{{k_{i}\choose v_{i}}{n_{i}-k_{i}\choose k_{i}-v_{i}}}{{n_{i}\choose k_{i}}}\right)g(\mathbf{v})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}g\left(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}\right),\end{split}

where
X_{i}
independently follows the hypergeometric distribution
H\mathcal{G}(n_{i},k_{i},k_{i})
.

The goal is to show

\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g\left(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}\right)\right]=1+o(1).

(60)

Under (
10
), there exists
\delta>0
,
b^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}\leq(2-\delta)\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}
, so

\lambda^{2}\asymp\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}}{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}.

(61)

To prove (
60
), we consider the value of
\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g\left(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}\right)\right]
on different events and the rest of the proof can be divided into three steps.

Step 1
. First let
E_{1}
be the value of
\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g\left(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}\right)\right]
on event
\{\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1\}
, in this step, we show
E_{1}=1+o(1)
. Notice

\begin{split}E_{1}&=\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d}\right)1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left({\rm{exp}}\left(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d}\right)\right)1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{Bin_{d}}\left({\rm{exp}}(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d})\right)1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&\overset{(c)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[\left(1+\tilde{q}_{d}\left(e^{\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}}-1\right)\right)^{k_{d}}1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&\overset{(d)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(k_{d}\tilde{q}_{d}(e^{\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}}-1)\right)1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)\right]\\
&\overset{(e)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(Bk_{d}q_{d}\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\right)\right],\end{split}

(62)

where
\tilde{q}_{d}=\frac{k_{d}}{n_{d}-k_{d}},q_{d}=\frac{k_{d}}{n_{d}}
. (a) is due to Lemma
6
(
72
),
(b)
is due to the stochastic dominance of binomial distributed random variable to hypergeometric distributed random variable( see Lemma 5.2 of
Butucea and
Ingster 2013
) and
{\rm{Bin}}_{d}
denotes the distribution of a binomial distribution
{\rm{Bin}}(k_{d},\frac{k_{d}}{n_{d}-k_{d}})
, (c) is due to the moment generating function of a Binomial distribution, (d) is due to the fact that
(1+x)\leq e^{x}
for any
x
and (e) is because condition on event
1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\leq 1)
, there exists
B>0
such that
(e^{\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}}-1)\leq B\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}
.

We can apply the argument in (
62
) sequentially and get an upper bound for
E_{1}
,

\begin{split}E_{1}&\leq\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-1}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(Bk_{d}q_{d}\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\right)\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d-2}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(Bk_{d}k_{d-1}q_{d}q_{d-1}\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-2}\right)\right]\\
&\ldots\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times H\mathcal{G}_{2}}\left[{\rm{exp}}\left(Bk_{3}\cdots k_{d-1}k_{d}q_{3}\cdots q_{d-1}q_{d}\lambda^{2}X_{1}X_{2}\right)\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}}[{\rm{exp}}(Bk_{2}\cdots k_{d}q_{2}\cdots q_{d}\lambda^{2}X_{1})]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\rm{Bin}}_{1}}[{\rm{exp}}(Bk_{2}\cdots k_{d}q_{2}\cdots q_{d}\lambda^{2}X_{1})]\\
&\leq\left(1+\tilde{q}_{1}(e^{Bk_{2}\cdots k_{d}q_{2}\cdots q_{d}\lambda^{2}}-1)\right)^{k_{2}}\\
&\leq{\rm{exp}}(Bk_{1}k_{2}\cdots k_{d}q_{1}q_{2}\cdots q_{d}\lambda^{2})\\
&=1+o(1),\end{split}

where
q_{i}=\frac{k_{i}}{n_{i}}
and the constant
B
may vary from line to line. The last equality is due to assumption in (
10
) that
\frac{\left(k_{1}\cdots k_{d}\right)^{2}}{n_{1}\ldots n_{d}}\lambda^{2}\to 0
.

Step 2.
To show (
60
), there is left to show

E_{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\geq 1)\right]=o(1).

(63)

In this step, we show there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that
E_{2}=o(1)
on any of event
\{X_{i}\leq\delta_{1}k_{i}\}
,
1\leq i\leq d
.

Denote
\mathbb{P}_{i}(x):=\mathbb{P}_{H\mathcal{G}_{i}}(X_{i}=x)=\frac{{k_{i}\choose x}{n_{i}-k_{i}\choose k_{i}-x}}{{n_{i}\choose k_{i}}}
. Since (
61
) and (
9
), we can find small enough
\delta_{1}
such that

\delta_{1}\lambda^{2}\frac{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}{k_{i}}\leq\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})/2\quad\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,d.

(64)

So by the definition in (
59
) and (
72
) of Lemma
6
, we have

\begin{split}E_{2}=&\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}[g(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\geq 1)]\\
\leq&\underset{v_{1}\cdots v_{d}\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}{\sum_{v_{1}=0}^{k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=0}^{k_{d}}}{\rm{exp}}(\lambda^{2}v_{1}\cdots v_{d})\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{j}(v_{j}).\end{split}

(65)

To analysis the above term, we again consider its value on different events. First we consider the value of
E_{2}
on event
W_{1}=\{X_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1}\}
. Notice on
W_{1}\bigcap\{\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\geq 1\}
, for sufficient small
\delta_{1}
,

\begin{split}X_{2}&\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\delta_{1}k_{1}X_{3}\cdots X_{d-1}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\delta_{1}k_{1}k_{3}\cdots k_{d-1}}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})},\end{split}

(66)

where the least inequality is due to (
61
) and (
9
). So by Lemma
7
(with
p=\frac{k_{2}}{n_{2}},r(p)=\log\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}
), for
v_{2}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{2}(v_{2})\leq{\rm{exp}}\left(-v_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)(1+o(1))\right).

(67)

So on
W_{1}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{H\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times H\mathcal{G}_{d}}\left[g(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})1(\lambda^{2}X_{1}\cdots X_{d-1}\geq 1)1(X_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1})\right]\\
\leq&\sum_{v_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1}}\sum_{v_{2}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}}\sum_{v_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{exp}}(\lambda^{2}v_{1}\cdots v_{d})\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{j}(v_{j})\\
\leq&\sum_{v_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1}}\sum_{v_{2}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}}\sum_{v_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{exp}}(\lambda^{2}v_{1}\cdots v_{d}){\rm{exp}}\left(-v_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\left(1+o(1)\right)\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{v_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1}}\sum_{v_{2}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}}\sum_{v_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{exp}}\left(v_{2}(\lambda^{2}v_{1}v_{3}\cdots v_{d}-\log\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})\left(1+o(1)\right)\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{v_{1}\leq\delta_{1}k_{1}}\sum_{v_{2}\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}}\sum_{v_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}\cdots\sum_{v_{d}=1}^{k_{d}}{\rm{exp}}\left(v_{2}(\delta_{1}\lambda^{2}k_{1}k_{3}\cdots k_{d}-\log\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})\left(1+o(1)\right)\right)\\
\leq&\breve{\mathbf{k}}{\rm{exp}}\left(-\frac{1}{2}v_{2}\log\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\to 0,\end{split}

(68)

where the last inequality is due to (
64
) and the last term goes to 0 is because
v_{2}\log\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\gg\log(\breve{\mathbf{k}})
.

By the same argument of (
68
), we can show
E_{2}
goes to 0 on any of the event
\{X_{i}\leq\delta_{1}k_{i}\}
for
2\leq i\leq d
.

Step 3.
In this step, we show the value of
E_{2}
is
o(1)
on event

\mathcal{H}=\left\{(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}):X_{1}\geq\delta_{1}k_{1},X_{2}\geq\delta_{1}k_{2},\ldots,X_{d}\geq\delta_{1}k_{d}\right\}.

(69)

Combining this results with the results in Step 2, we have shown (
63
).

We decompose
\mathcal{H}
into two part
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and consider the value of
E_{2}
on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
separately. Here
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
are defined as

\begin{split}\mathcal{H}_{1}=\left\{(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})\in\mathcal{H}:\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{1}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\geq 2\rho_{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\right)\right\},\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}=\left\{(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})\in\mathcal{H}:\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{1}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})<2\rho_{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\right)\right\},\end{split}

(70)

where
\rho_{\mathbf{v}}:=\frac{\breve{\mathbf{v}}}{\breve{\mathbf{k}}}
.

Denote the value of
E_{2}
on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
as
E_{21}
and
E_{22}
, respectively. We first bound
E_{21}
.

Observe that
\rho_{\mathbf{v}}\geq\delta_{1}^{d}
for
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}
. Recall (
58
), observe that we could take
\delta>0
small enough such that
t=T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}-b(1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}})<0
. Applying Lemma
6
(
73
) we have

\begin{split}E_{21}&\leq\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}}{\rm{exp}}\left(-(T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}-b)^{2}+\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}\right)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{j}(v_{j})\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}}{\rm{exp}}\left(-(T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}-b)^{2}+\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}+o(T^{2}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\right),\end{split}

(71)

where the second inequality is due to Lemma
7
and observe that
X_{i}\geq\frac{k_{i}}{\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}}\,(1\leq i\leq d)
.

Note that when
\delta>0
is small enough, we can take
\delta_{2}=\delta_{2}(\delta)>0
such that
(T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}-b)^{2}\geq\delta_{2}T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}
for the first term at the right hand side of (
71
). Recall
T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}\sim 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\right)
, so on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
,

\begin{split}&\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}+o(T^{2}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\\
=&\frac{2\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}+o(T^{2}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\\
\leq&\left(\frac{1}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-1\right)\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log(\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})+o(T^{2}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\leq o(T^{2}_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}),\end{split}

here the first inequality is due to the construction of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
.
Therefore

E_{21}\leq\breve{\mathbf{k}}{\rm{exp}}\left(-(\delta_{2}+o(1))T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}\right)=o(1).

Now we consider
E_{22}
. Recall (
55
), (
58
) and
z_{\mathbf{v}}^{2}=\rho_{\mathbf{v}}\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}=\rho_{\mathbf{v}}(2-\delta)(\sum_{i=1}^{d}k_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}})
, observe that on
\mathcal{H}_{2}
, for small enough
\delta,\delta_{1}
, we have
T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-2z_{\mathbf{v}}<0
. So by Lemma
6
(
74
), we have

\begin{split}E_{22}&\leq\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}}{\rm{exp}}\left(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}/2-(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-z_{\mathbf{v}})^{2}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{j}(v_{j})\\
&\leq\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}}{\rm{exp}}\left(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}/2-(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-z_{\mathbf{v}})^{2}-\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\right)\right),\end{split}

where the last inequality is by Lemma
7
.

Since
\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\sim T^{2}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}/2
, the power in the exponent is of the form

-(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-z_{\mathbf{v}})^{2}+o(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}).

Result
E_{22}=o(1)
is due to the following result by observing (
58
) and (
55
)

\begin{split}T^{2}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-z^{2}_{\mathbf{v}}=2\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}-(2-\delta)\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}\frac{\prod_{j\neq i}v_{i}}{\prod_{j\neq i}k_{i}}\log\frac{n_{i}}{k_{i}}\geq\delta^{\prime}T^{2}_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}},\end{split}

for some
\delta^{\prime}>0
.

So we have shown
E_{21}=o(1)
,
E_{22}=o(1)
. This has finished the proof.

Lemma 6

Let
z^{2}_{\mathbf{v}}=\lambda^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}
.

(1)

g(\mathbf{v})\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))\right)={\rm{exp}}(z_{\mathbf{v}}^{2})=:g_{1}(\mathbf{v}).

(72)

(2) If
b\geq\frac{T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}
, then

\begin{split}g(\mathbf{v})&\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}},{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}\}}\right)\\
&\leq{\rm{exp}}(-(T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}-b)^{2}+\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}^{2}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}})=:g_{2}(\mathbf{v})\end{split}

(73)

(3) Let
v_{1}\geq\delta_{1}k_{1},\ldots,v_{d}\geq\delta_{1}k_{d}
and
T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\leq 2z_{\mathbf{v}}
, then

\begin{split}g(\mathbf{v})\leq&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\leq T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}}\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}^{2}/2-(T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}-z_{\mathbf{v}})^{2})=:g_{3}(\mathbf{v})\end{split}

(74)

Lemma 7
( Lemma 5.3 of
Butucea and
Ingster 2013
)

Let
X_{i}\sim H\mathcal{G}(n_{i},k_{i},k_{i})
and denote
\mathbb{P}_{i}(x):=\mathbb{P}_{H\mathcal{G}}(X_{i}=x)=\frac{{k_{i}\choose x}{n_{i}-k_{i}\choose k_{i}-x}}{{n_{i}\choose k_{i}}}
. Let
k_{i}\to\infty
,
p\to 0
,
v_{i}\geq k_{i}/r(p)
where
r(p)\geq 1
for
p>0
small enough, and
\log(r(p))=o(log(p^{-1}))
. Then

\log(\mathbb{P}_{i}(v_{i}))\leq v_{i}\log(p)(1+o(1)).

B.1.4
Proof of Lemma
6

The proof is based on the following fact: if
X\sim N(0,1)
, then
\mathbb{E}({\rm{exp}}(\tau X))={\rm{exp}}(\tau^{2}/2)
.

We start with the proof of (
72
). Given two possible latent supports for the signal
C_{1},C_{2}
, define
V_{1}=C_{1}\setminus C_{2}
,
V_{2}=C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
,
V=C_{1}\bigcap C_{2}
. Notice
V_{1},V_{2},V
are disjoint and
\#(V_{1})=\#(V_{2})=\breve{\mathbf{k}}-\breve{\mathbf{v}}
,
\#(V)=\breve{\mathbf{v}}
, here
\breve{\mathbf{v}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{\mathbf{v}}_{i}
.

Recall
\rho_{\mathbf{v}}:=\frac{\breve{\mathbf{v}}}{\breve{\mathbf{k}}},b^{2}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{k}}
,
z_{\mathbf{v}}=\lambda^{2}\breve{\mathbf{v}}=b^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}}
and the definition of
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{C}^{sum}
in the proof of Theorem
9
, so

\begin{split}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{C_{1}}^{sum}&=\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{1}}^{sum}+\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\\
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{C_{2}}^{sum}&=\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{2}}^{sum}+\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}.\end{split}

So

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))\right)&={\rm{exp}}(-b^{2})\mathbb{E}_{0}(b\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{1}}^{sum}+b\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{2}}^{sum}+2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum})\\
&={\rm{exp}}(b^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}})={\rm{exp}}(z_{\mathbf{v}}^{2}).\end{split}

The first inequality of (
73
) is trivial and we focus on the second one. Let
h\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}},{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}\}}\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}h)\\
&\times\mathbb{E}_{0}({\rm{exp}}\left((b-h)({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}})+h({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}-2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\right)1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}},{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}\leq T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}\}})\\
\leq&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}h)\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}\left((b-h)({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}})\right)\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}h)\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}\left((b-h)\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V_{2}})+2(b-h)\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\right)\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}\left(-b^{2}+2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}h+(b-h)^{2}(1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}})+2(b-h)^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}}\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}\left(-b^{2}+2T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}h+(b-h)^{2}(1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}})\right).\end{split}

Plug in
h=b-\frac{T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}}}{1+\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}
, we get the result.

To prove (
74
), for
h\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}+b({\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{1}}+{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{C_{2}}))1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}\leq T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}}\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2})\mathbb{E}_{0}(b\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{1}}^{sum}+b\sqrt{1-\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V_{2}}^{sum}+2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}\leq T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}})\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}}+T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}h)\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}\left(\left(2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-h\right){\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}+h({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}})\right)1_{\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{sum}_{V}\leq T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}\}}\right)\\
\leq&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}}+T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}h)\mathbb{E}_{0}\left({\rm{exp}}\left((2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-h){\mathbfcal{Y}}_{V}^{sum}\right)\right)\\
=&{\rm{exp}}(-b^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{v}}+T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}h+(2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-h)^{2}/2).\end{split}

Plug in
h=2b\sqrt{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}}-T_{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}}
, we get the inequality.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
11

We adopt the same notation as in the proof of Theorem
9
. First note that

\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi_{{\rm{CHC}}_{D}}^{s}=1)\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi_{sum}=1)+\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi_{scan}=1)

and

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\psi_{{\rm{CHC}}_{D}}^{s}=0)\leq\min(\mathbb{P}_{\mathbfcal{X}}(\psi_{sum}=1),\mathbb{P}_{\mathbfcal{X}}(\psi_{scan}=1)).

So we could consider two tests
\psi_{sum}
and
\psi_{scan}
separately.

Let
T_{sum}=\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{n_{d}}{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}}{\sqrt{n_{1}\cdots n_{d}}}
and denote
Z_{\lambda}
to be a random variable following distribution
N(\lambda,1)
. Under
H_{0}
,
T_{sum}\sim N(0,1)
,
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi_{sum}=1)=\Phi(-W)\to 0
, since
W\to\infty
by assumption.
Similarly we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi_{scan}=1)&=\mathbb{P}_{0}(T_{scan}>T_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{n}})\leq G_{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathbf{n}}\mathbb{P}_{0}(Z_{0}>\sqrt{2\log(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}})})\\
&\leq G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\rm{exp}}(-\log(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}))/(2\log(G^{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathbf{k}}))\to 0.\end{split}

Here the second inequality is because
